Well, um... I guess I usually start out thinking I'm just a little hungry, you know? Just wanting a quick bite. But... I want to make sure I'm being completely honest with you, since you're trying to help me. 

I guess what worries me a little bit is that once I start picking at the food late at night, I'll actually tell myself, "Okay, that's enough, Santiago." Like, I know I should put it away. But... my hand just keeps going back for more. It almost feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense? Like my brain says stop, but my hand just isn't listening. 

I know it probably sounds a little silly, a grown man saying he can't stop eating some chips or whatever. I really hope it doesn't make me sound too crazy or anything. I'm sure it's just a bad habit I need to break. Is that... does that answer what you were asking me? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

I don't know if that makes sense, or if I'm explaining it right... is that the kind of thing you help people with here?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating during the day, or is it just at night that you feel like your hand keeps going back for food?\n\nPatient: Oh, during the day I think I'm pretty good. I work in shipping and receiving, so I'm usually pretty busy on my feet and just eat a normal lunch on my break. I haven't really noticed it happening then... at least I don't think so. \n\nIt's really just at night when I'm home. I mean, I'm sure everybody likes to unwind with a little snack in the evening, right? I just... maybe let it go a bit too far sometimes. I guess I just sort of hurry through it? \n\nIt's almost like I want to finish it before anyone wakes up and comes into the kitchen. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. It's not like I have this one specific weakness or anything. I mean, I guess whatever is just around in the pantry? 

Like I said, maybe some chips, or maybe some cookies if we have them... just normal snack stuff, you know? It's not like I'm going out of my way to buy anything special or... or going crazy over one specific kind of food. I just kind of grab whatever is easiest when I'm sitting there watching TV. 

I think that's pretty typical, right? Just eating whatever's convenient? I hope that's what you were asking. I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is, because it's really just normal pantry stuff. Does that make sense?

Well, um, I think it's kind of like what I was saying earlier... it's really just whatever happens to be in the house. It's almost like I don't get to make the decision anymore? I don't know if that makes sense, or if I'm explaining it right... is that the kind of thing you help people with here?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating during the day, or is it just at night that you feel like your hand keeps going back for food?\n\nPatient: Oh, during the day I think I'm pretty good. I haven't really noticed it happening then... at least I don't think so. \n\nIt's really just at night when I'm home. I mean, I'm sure everybody likes to unwind with a little snack in the evening, right? I just... maybe let it go a bit too far sometimes. I hope I'm not making a big deal out of nothing, I know it's probably just a bad habit. But yeah, daytime feels pretty normal and under control to me. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. I mean... I haven't really noticed feeling actual guilt about it, like I committed a crime or did something terrible. Is that... is that the kind of information you need?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly or eating more than you intended to during these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I might eat a little faster than normal? I mean, it's not like I'm shoveling it in or anything crazy. It's just... you know, when I'm sitting down for dinner with the family, we take our time, we talk. But at night, when everyone else is asleep... I guess what worries me a little bit is that once I start picking at the food, it's hard to just walk away. \n\nI'll tell myself, \"Okay, that's enough, Santiago, time for bed,\" but... it's almost like my hand just keeps going back to the bag or the box. Like I don't really get to make the decision to stop anymore, even though I know I should. It's probably just a lack of willpower on my part, you know? I'm sure it's nothing too serious. \n\nBut yeah, I guess it does feel a little hard to stop once I get going. Is that... is that normal for people who snack at night? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during the day, like when you're having your normal lunch at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. During the day, I'm... I'm completely fine, actually. I mean, at work, I just have my regular lunch in the breakroom with the other guys from the warehouse. I eat my sandwich or whatever I packed, and then I just go right back out to the floor. \n\nI don't have any trouble stopping then. It's very routine, you know? I just eat my normal portion and I'm full, and I don't even really think about food again until my shift is over. I'm totally in control during the day, I promise. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I'm not really a stressed-out kind of guy, you know? My job is pretty steady, and I like keeping busy. And I wouldn't say I'm anxious or anything like that. \n\nI guess maybe sometimes it's just... I don't know, maybe a little bit of boredom? Like, the house is quiet, everyone's asleep, and I'm just sitting there watching TV. It's just something to do with my hands, I suppose. But I wouldn't say I'm eating because I'm upset or anything negative. \n\nIt's just a little habit I've picked up, I think. A lot of guys my age probably do the same thing, right? Just having a little snack while watching a late-night game or a movie. I hope that makes sense. I just don't want you to think I'm, like, emotionally eating or anything serious like that. Does that sound like a normal reason to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you couldn't stop eating during a meal at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, definitely not. Like I think I was saying just a little bit ago, work is... well, it's totally fine. I've never had that feeling during my shifts. \n\nI just eat what I brought and get right back out to the loading dock. There's no urge to keep going or anything like that. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But yeah, work is a hundred percent normal for me. I wouldn't let myself get out of hand in front of the guys anyway, you know? \n\nDoes that make sense for what you're looking for? I'm trying to give you the best answers I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating more than you intended to during these late-night snacking episodes because you can't stop or because you're just really hungry?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I usually start out thinking I'm just a little hungry, you know? Just wanting a quick bite. But... I want to make sure I'm being completely honest with you, since you're trying to help me. \n\nI guess what worries me a little bit is that once I start picking at the food late at night, I'll actually tell myself, \"Okay, that's enough, Santiago.\" Like, I know I should put it away. But... my hand just keeps going back for more. It almost feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense? Like my brain says stop, but my hand just isn't listening. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly, a grown man saying he can't stop eating some chips or whatever. I really hope it doesn't make me sound too crazy or anything. I'm sure it's just a bad habit I need to break. Is that... does that answer what you were asking me? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during the daytime, outside of your late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, no... no, definitely not during the day. Like I was saying about work, I just have my regular lunch and that's it. I'm usually too busy moving around with the inventory and the trucks to even think about snacking, to be honest. \n\nSo I've never felt out of control like that when the sun's up. It's really just... just that late-night thing when the house is quiet and I'm sitting still. I hope that's a good sign? Like, maybe it means it's not too bad since I can manage perfectly fine during the daytime. Am I giving you the right kind of answers for what you need? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it kind of goes back to what I was trying to explain just a little bit ago. And I really hate admitting this, because I like to think I'm a pretty together guy, you know? But... no, not really. \n\nDuring those late nights, I'll literally say out loud to myself, 'Santiago, that's enough, you don't need any more.' But my hand just keeps reaching into the bag anyway. It's like I'm not the one making the choice to keep eating, even though I'm the one doing it. \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous repeating that. I definitely don't want you to think I'm totally out of control in my normal life or anything—it's really just those quiet moments at night. I just want to make sure I'm being as honest as you need me to be so you can do your job. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night snacking episodes, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe just a little bit? I mean, I think anyone would feel a little silly after eating too many chips, right? \n\nI guess sometimes I wake up the next morning and see the empty wrappers in the trash, and I feel a little... I don't know, a little foolish, maybe? Like I should have known better. But it's not like it ruins my whole day or anything. I just kind of brush it off and head to work. \n\nI know it's not the best habit, and I'm definitely going to try harder to stop. So I just have a plate like everyone else and leave it at that. \n\nIt's really just that quiet time at night when I'm by myself. During the day or at special events, I'm totally fine. I hope that's a good thing? Like, it means it's not a huge problem, right, if it's only happening when I'm alone watching TV?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you lose control over eating with all types of food or just certain ones during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I think it's kind of like what I was saying earlier... it's really just whatever happens to be in the house. I wouldn't say it's all types of food, like I'm not eating a whole meal or anything, and it's definitely not one specific thing I'm obsessed with. It's just... whatever is easy to grab out of the pantry. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have a more specific answer for you. I just don't want to make it sound like I'm hoarding special junk food or doing anything too crazy. It's really just regular snacks, whatever is closest when I sit down. I'm trying to give you the best picture of it... does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you lose control over eating at any other time of day besides late at night?\n\nPatient: No, honestly, it's really just a nighttime thing. During the day, you know, I'm at the warehouse, I'm busy, I just eat a regular lunch with the guys. I don't even really think about food that much when I'm working or out and about. \n\nIt's really only when I'm finally home and the day is done. I'd never do that kind of thing during the day anyway, I mean, I wouldn't want anyone at work to see me just eating and eating. That would be pretty embarrassing, right? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I wouldn't say it's causing, like, *major* problems. I mean, I'm still getting to all my shifts at the warehouse on time, and I'm handling all the shipping and receiving stuff just fine. My boss hasn't noticed anything slipping, so that's good, right? 

But, um... I guess maybe it's affecting things just a tiny bit at home. Like, I've started sort of... avoiding going into the kitchen late at night if my family is still up in there. I just... I worry someone might notice that I'm acting a little uneasy or weird around the food, you know? I really don't want them to think less of me or worry about me. So I just kind of steer clear until they go to bed. 

It's nothing huge, though! I'm still doing what I need to do during the day. Does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.

It's really just... just that late-night thing when the house is quiet and I'm sitting still. I hope that's a good sign? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I definitely don't want you to think I'm totally out of control in my normal life or anything—it's really just those quiet moments at night. I just want to make sure I'm being as honest as you need me to be so you can do your job. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night snacking episodes, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe just a little bit? I don't know, a little foolish, maybe? Like I should have known better. But it's not like it ruins my whole day or anything.
